  "domain_models": [
    {
      "DOMAIN_MODEL_ID": "ESA-DM01",
      "TITLE": "Architectural significance and concerns",
      "PROPERTY_IDS": [
        "ESA-001",
        "ESA-002",
        "ESA-003",
        "ESA-004",
        "ESA-005",
        "ESA-006",
        "ESA-056"
      ],
      "OBJECTS_AND_STATE": "A software system, its environment, stakeholder concerns, candidate choices, affected obligations and the evidence believed to support them.",
      "RELATIONAL_ACCOUNT": "A choice is significant relative to the reach of its consequences, not a fixed file size or job role. Concerns attach to choices through scenarios/constraints. An authorised decision and an implemented result remain separate state changes.",
      "OPERATION_AND_COMMUNICATION": "Users, maintainers, operators and sponsors supply concerns; designers explain consequences; actual authorised participants accept trade-offs; implementers change real artefacts.",
      "VALIDITY_AND_OBSERVATION": "Trace a claimed concern through a selected choice to an observation. Agreement shows coordination, not successful operation.",
      "MINIMAL_CONFIGURATION": "A short conversation, a few explicit constraints and a check of the real effect can be coherent for one team.",
      "DISCRIMINATING_EXAMPLE": "Analytical: choosing a colour token is normally local, but a colour-encoding decision becomes architectural when every alarm channel and accessibility commitment depends on it.",
      "SOURCE_IDS": [
        "ESA-S002",
        "ESA-S010",
        "ESA-S011",
        "ESA-S017",
        "ESA-S020",
        "ESA-S035"
      ],
      "EVIDENCE_STATUS": "ANALYTICAL_RECONSTRUCTION_GROUNDED_IN_NAMED_SOURCES; not an independently validated unified formalism."
    },
    {
      "DOMAIN_MODEL_ID": "ESA-DM02",
      "TITLE": "Decomposition and interface contracts",
      "PROPERTY_IDS": [
        "ESA-007",
        "ESA-008",
        "ESA-009",
        "ESA-010",
        "ESA-011",
        "ESA-012",
        "ESA-013",
        "ESA-025",
        "ESA-061",
        "ESA-065",
        "ESA-066",
        "ESA-068"
      ],
      "OBJECTS_AND_STATE": "Responsibilities, hidden decisions, model meanings, interfaces, connectors, shared resources and typed dependencies.",
      "RELATIONAL_ACCOUNT": "Information hiding partitions knowledge; connector semantics govern interaction; containment constrains propagation. A module boundary, domain context, deployment boundary and team boundary are related but non-identical partitions.",
      "OPERATION_AND_COMMUNICATION": "Producers and consumers negotiate meaningful obligations, implement or adapt interfaces, and exercise change/failure cases across them.",
      "VALIDITY_AND_OBSERVATION": "An import graph establishes only its extracted relation. Correctness dependence, shared state and external effects require additional evidence. Wright provides a conditional formal branch, not automatic semantic closure.",
      "MINIMAL_CONFIGURATION": "A direct local interface with explicit meaning and a few integration cases; no network boundary is required.",
      "DISCRIMINATING_EXAMPLE": "Analytical: two services with separate repositories share a global database trigger; their apparent isolation fails when one change alters the other’s invariant.",
      "SOURCE_IDS": [
        "ESA-S001",
        "ESA-S006",
        "ESA-S015",
        "ESA-S026",
        "ESA-S033",
        "ESA-S034",
        "ESA-S035"
      ],
      "EVIDENCE_STATUS": "ANALYTICAL_RECONSTRUCTION_GROUNDED_IN_NAMED_SOURCES; not an independently validated unified formalism."
    },
    {
      "DOMAIN_MODEL_ID": "ESA-DM03",
      "TITLE": "Quality-attribute trade-offs",
      "PROPERTY_IDS": [
        "ESA-014",
        "ESA-015",
        "ESA-016",
        "ESA-017",
        "ESA-018",
        "ESA-019",
        "ESA-055",
        "ESA-062"
      ],
      "OBJECTS_AND_STATE": "Concern-specific scenarios, response measures, constraints, preferences, mechanisms and environmental assumptions.",
      "RELATIONAL_ACCOUNT": "A tactic changes a quality model parameter, not an abstract quality in isolation. The system response depends on interaction with other tactics and workload. Hard limits restrict acceptable options before preference comparison.",
      "OPERATION_AND_COMMUNICATION": "Stakeholders define acceptable responses; designers propose interventions; evaluators construct models/tests; operators supply observations.",
      "VALIDITY_AND_OBSERVATION": "Use workload, fault and population scope and a declared accounting boundary. The same functional answer can be unacceptable because of latency, information disclosure or resource use.",
      "MINIMAL_CONFIGURATION": "One concrete scenario and competing-cost check are sufficient for a narrow reversible decision.",
      "DISCRIMINATING_EXAMPLE": "Published: EcoFaaS’s chosen energy/tail-latency benefits coexist with worse mean latency versus its full-frequency baseline; its energy boundary is package/DRAM, not total carbon.",
      "SOURCE_IDS": [
        "ESA-S016",
        "ESA-S017",
        "ESA-S018",
        "ESA-S020",
        "ESA-S022",
        "ESA-S061"
      ],
      "EVIDENCE_STATUS": "ANALYTICAL_RECONSTRUCTION_GROUNDED_IN_NAMED_SOURCES; not an independently validated unified formalism."
    },
    {
      "DOMAIN_MODEL_ID": "ESA-DM04",
      "TITLE": "Views and correspondence",
      "PROPERTY_IDS": [
        "ESA-003",
        "ESA-020",
        "ESA-021",
        "ESA-022",
        "ESA-023",
        "ESA-024",
        "ESA-041",
        "ESA-059"
      ],
      "OBJECTS_AND_STATE": "Views of structure, interaction, deployment/development organisation, their viewpoint rules and mappings to realised artefacts.",
      "RELATIONAL_ACCOUNT": "A view selects facts for a concern. Correspondences connect elements/relations across views and to implementation; consistency among views is weaker than truth about implementation. Recovery produces hypotheses with provenance.",
      "OPERATION_AND_COMMUNICATION": "Different consumers ask different questions. Authors map shared elements and explain differences; analysts inspect source, configuration or traces to check relevant claims.",
      "VALIDITY_AND_OBSERVATION": "Formal semantics can establish a model property; relation extraction and implementation refinement have separate obligations. A sampled trace cannot certify every possible runtime path.",
      "MINIMAL_CONFIGURATION": "Use the least number of views that answer the concern, with explicit labels and a manual correspondence check.",
      "DISCRIMINATING_EXAMPLE": "Analytical: a logical service view is consistent with a deployment drawing but both omit a production sidecar that changes network and failure behaviour.",
      "SOURCE_IDS": [
        "ESA-S003",
        "ESA-S011",
        "ESA-S014",
        "ESA-S015",

      "OBJECTS_AND_STATE": "Feasible alternatives including no change, architectural drivers, scenarios, assumptions, risk/sensitivity findings and costs.",
      "RELATIONAL_ACCOUNT": "SAAM emphasises changes; ATAM exposes multi-quality risks; CBAM examines utility/cost under assumptions; prototypes and formal models supply different evidence. They can substitute or complement, not automatically all execute.",
      "OPERATION_AND_COMMUNICATION": "Analysis, design synthesis and evaluation interleave: a risk may change requirements, an experiment may reveal a new option, and a local design may proceed while another uncertainty is studied.",
      "VALIDITY_AND_OBSERVATION": "Method outputs are not outcomes. Compare alternatives under equivalent conditions and preserve exclusions, weak baselines and uncertainty.",
      "MINIMAL_CONFIGURATION": "A focused alternative-and-risk conversation with one discriminating experiment can be enough.",
      "DISCRIMINATING_EXAMPLE": "Analytical: a successful review is followed by a workload dominated by an omitted write path; the previous non-risk is reopened, not defended by the review’s approval.",

      "OBJECTS_AND_STATE": "Current decisions, rationale, assumptions, status/history and the people whose work or commitments depend on them.",
      "RELATIONAL_ACCOUNT": "Decision dependencies make knowledge transfer useful. Communication requirements follow relevant technical/work coupling, but neither a record nor an organisation chart mechanically produces coherent design.",
      "OPERATION_AND_COMMUNICATION": "Teams exchange decisions and exceptions; later actors retrieve rationale; authorised participants revise it when assumptions fail. Reconstructed explanation remains separate from documentary history.",
      "VALIDITY_AND_OBSERVATION": "Check a real later question and a real cross-team dependency. Surveys and observational congruence are not universal causal evidence.",
      "MINIMAL_CONFIGURATION": "One shared explanation and a short retained note for a non-obvious decision; no board or permanent extra role is inherent.",
      "DISCRIMINATING_EXAMPLE": "Analytical: a successor can explain why an interface was chosen but discovers a critical workload assumption no longer holds; the old record guides revision rather than preventing it.",

      "OBJECTS_AND_STATE": "Baseline constraints, extracted/observed relations, mappings, deviations, evidence and adjudicated dispositions.",
      "RELATIONAL_ACCOUNT": "A conformance result relates one mapping and dependency kind to one baseline. A smell is a candidate risk signal. The target of correction may be code, configuration, description, rule or an unsupported inference.",
      "OPERATION_AND_COMMUNICATION": "Analysts report findings; maintainers explain context; authorised reviewers evaluate consequences and change the right artefact. Rechecking establishes whether the actual disposition took effect.",
      "VALIDITY_AND_OBSERVATION": "Positive and negative cases expose missed edges and false alarms. Harm requires a current invariant/quality/burden argument, not mere divergence.",

      "STRONGEST_OBJECTION": "“Architecture is important decisions” can become circular and give architects unlimited jurisdiction.",

      "RESPONSE_OR_DERIVATIVE": "Use consequence-bound significance and a named consumer rather than rank or diagram altitude.",
      "LATER_FINDINGS": "ESA-006 has no general operational property; ESA-001 survives in a bounded form; conceptual integrity does not require central approval.",
      "PRESENT_JUDGEMENT": "ESA-006 has no general operational property; ESA-001 survives in a bounded form; conceptual integrity does not require central approval.",
      "RESIDUAL_UNCERTAINTY": "No universal threshold separates architecture from detailed design.",
      "EVOLUTION_CLASS": "NARROWED",
      "ANALYTICAL_STATUS": "Sourced objection/evidence distinguished from this packet’s present synthesis; not every proposed response is an empirically validated intervention.",
      "AUTHOR_REASSESSMENT": "The circularity objection defeats a universal jurisdiction claim, not every broad architecture definition. A decision-specific consequence is the surviving discriminator; no universal significance threshold is invented."

## GENEALOGY_AND_CRITICISM.md

Source: `SSS-L1-GENEALOGY_AND_CRITICISM` — `GENEALOGY_AND_CRITICISM.md`; exact bytes and SHA-256 in [source catalogue](../SOURCES.md#s-4ecd04650901dd14).

# Genealogy and criticism — complete selected navigation

Author candidate; historical transmission and present analytical relations are distinguished. Full selected nodes/edges/criticisms remain in the coverage carrier; all native content is preserved with exact corrections.

## Historical nodes

### ESA-G01

**Structured abstraction and layered responsibility** — 1968; inspected later transcription.

Reason about concurrent system behaviour and make implementation manageable. A hierarchy of levels and carefully delimited abstractions organise a small multiprogramming system.

Contribution: An early concrete account of organising software through abstraction rather than a later architecture-description notation.

Limit: One author-reported system and partial testing; not evidence that all modern layers yield reliability.

Sources: ESA-S005. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/0`.

### ESA-G02

**Information hiding, uses relations and program families** — 1972; 1976 bibliographic anchor; 1979.

Processing-step decomposition exposes design choices and makes anticipated changes propagate. Assign responsibilities around hidden decisions; distinguish uses from calls and plan feasible family extension/contraction.

Contribution: A semantic criterion for decomposition and an architectural family/change account.

Limit: Change hypotheses can be wrong; the KWIC illustration is not an industrial causal estimate. The 1976 item was inspected as metadata only.

Sources: ESA-S001, ESA-S006, ESA-S064. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/1`.

### ESA-G03

**Conceptual integrity and realistic design explanation** — 1986 inspected texts; 1975 book an uninspected historical lead.

Incoherent concepts and an idealised sequential account make design hard to communicate or maintain. Seek conceptual coherence and preserve a rationalised explanation while accepting iteration and discovery.

Contribution: Integrity is a system-level concern; explanatory order need not match the actual path of invention.

Limit: The 1975 monograph was not read in this run. Neither coherence nor retrospective rationalisation licenses one-person control or false provenance.

Sources: ESA-S008, ESA-S009, ESA-S010. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/2`.

### ESA-G04

**Software architecture as a distinct research object: structures, styles and connectors** — 1992–1994.

Programming-level accounts do not sufficiently describe consequential overall organisation. Describe elements, form and rationale; classify recurring component-and-connector organisations and combinations.

Contribution: A plural research programme for system structures, interactions and quality consequences.

Limit: Styles overlap and combine; categorisation is not proof of a best architecture.

Sources: ESA-S002, ESA-S004. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/3`.

### ESA-G05

**Multiple views and concern-selected representation** — 1995 onwards.

A single structural drawing cannot serve every stakeholder and design question. Use views with distinct concerns and semantics; scenarios expose their interaction; keep correspondences explicit.

Contribution: 4+1 is a worked view scheme, not a claim that all five artefacts are necessary everywhere.

Limit: Views can disagree, become stale or omit runtime/configuration relations.

Sources: ESA-S003, ESA-S014, ESA-S011. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/4`.

### ESA-G06

**ADLs and formal connector reasoning** — 1990s; 1997 paper/1998 inspected revision; 2000 taxonomy.

Informal interaction arrows omit assumptions needed to reason about composition. Give components/connectors semantic models and check compatibility under well-formedness conditions.

Contribution: Wright supplies a conditional deadlock result and a counterexample to compatibility alone.

Limit: CSP model assumptions and implementation correspondence remain obligations; taxonomy completeness is historical, not a claim about every current tool.

Sources: ESA-S014, ESA-S015. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/5`.

### ESA-G07

**Scenario-based quality evaluation and economic reasoning** — SAAM 1994; ATAM 2000; CBAM 2001; QAW 2003; subsequent evaluations.

Quality slogans and a preferred design conceal trade-offs, risks and alternatives. Elicit scenarios; explore sensitivity and trade-offs; estimate utility/cost under assumptions.

Contribution: Structured evaluation can make quality reasoning and stakeholder differences explicit.

Limit: Selected scenarios may miss decisive conditions; risk reports are not downstream controlled outcomes; comparative evidence is small and mixed.

Sources: ESA-S019, ESA-S020, ESA-S022, ESA-S017, ESA-S021, ESA-S023. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/6`.

### ESA-G08

**Architectural decisions, rationale and compact records** — 2005 inspected report; 2005 decision-paper abstract; 2011 ADR article.

Later actors see structures but cannot recover why alternatives were rejected. Preserve context, decision status and consequences; maintain supersession and links where useful.

Contribution: Decision knowledge supplements structural descriptions and can aid handover/reconsideration.

Limit: Survey perceptions do not prove benefit; the Archium paper was abstract-only and does not support detailed tool claims.

Sources: ESA-S027, ESA-S028, ESA-S029. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/7`.

### ESA-G09

**Conformance, reconstruction, erosion and smell analysis** — 1995; 2022–2025.

The implemented dependency structure can diverge from intended design without being recognised. Map extracted relations to a high-level model; inspect convergence/divergence; investigate risks and context.

Contribution: Conformance is relative to mappings and rules, not a judgement that old intent is always correct.

Limit: Extraction omits semantics; smell/performance effects vary; code-review comments measure discussion rather than prevalence of harmful erosion.

Sources: ESA-S045, ESA-S046, ESA-S047, ESA-S048. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/8`.

### ESA-G10

**Socio-technical coordination and mirroring** — 1968; empirical studies 2008 and inspected 2011 working-paper revision.

The people who must coordinate may not match the consequential dependencies in their work. Compare task/software dependencies with observed communication; investigate organisational/design relationships.

Contribution: An architectural intersection with work coordination rather than an org-chart design algorithm.

Limit: Single-organisation and matched-product studies are observational and confounded; deterministic causal readings are unsupported.

Sources: ESA-S030, ESA-S031, ESA-S032. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/9`.

### ESA-G11

**Architecture-based runtime change and self-adaptation** — 1998–1999; Rainbow 2004; robustness 2014; industrial survey 2023.

Static descriptions cannot guide or explain structural changes during operation. Connect probes, models, policies and effectors; constrain changes and evaluate effects.

Contribution: Architecture becomes an operative runtime abstraction, not merely documentation.

Limit: Model-state corruption and silent observation failures can defeat decisions; prototypes/surveys are not general autonomy evidence.

Sources: ESA-S051, ESA-S052, ESA-S053, ESA-S054, ESA-S055. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/10`.

### ESA-G12

**Reusable components, software families and architectural mismatch** — 1968 event/1969 report; 1995 case; institutional collection inspected 2026.

Reuse and interchangeability promise leverage, but compatible-looking parts carry hidden assumptions. Component reuse must reconcile control, data, state and platform assumptions; family architecture preserves commonality/variation.

Contribution: McIlroy’s opening supplies an early aspiration; Aesop provides a concrete negative integration experience.

Limit: The McIlroy opening and collection entry descriptions do not support uninspected implementation details or economic outcomes. Aesop publications share one programme.

Sources: ESA-S007, ESA-S026, ESA-S066. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/11`.

### ESA-G13

**Domain-driven design intersection: bounded semantic models** — 2003 book as historical lead; inspected author reference dated 2015.

Different business contexts require different meanings for apparently shared terms. Bound a model’s applicability and map explicit relationships/translations between contexts.

Contribution: A semantic boundary is distinct from a code module or deployment boundary.

Limit: The original book was not read; bounded contexts are not automatically services and translations have costs.

Sources: ESA-S033, ESA-S036. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/12`.

### ESA-G14

**REST as a constrained hypermedia architectural style** — 2000 dissertation.

Distributed hypermedia needs evolvability, scalability and interactions among independently developed participants. Combine client/server, stateless interaction, cacheability, uniform interface, layering and optional code-on-demand.

Contribution: Uniform-interface constraints include resource identification, representations, self-descriptive messages and hypermedia application state.

Limit: A generic interface sacrifices some application-specific efficiency; REST is not every HTTP service and statelessness does not remove persistent resource state.

Sources: ESA-S034. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/13`.

### ESA-G15

**Service-oriented reference modelling** — OASIS Reference Model 1.0, 2006.

Services cross ownership and access boundaries and need a common conceptual account. Relate visibility, interaction, service descriptions, execution context and real-world effects.

Contribution: Awareness, willingness and reachability are not interchangeable with authorisation or successful effect.

Limit: A reference model does not prescribe one stack or establish service economics.

Sources: ESA-S035. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/14`.

### ESA-G16

**Microservices, modular monoliths and consolidation** — 2014 account; 2015 caution; 2019 and 2020 field cases; 2024–2026 research.

Large products may need differentiated deployment and team change, but distributed boundaries introduce operational coordination. Select deployment boundaries according to genuine independence needs; preserve modularity or consolidate when boundaries supply little benefit.

Contribution: Shopify and Istio show that purposeful internal modules or consolidation can be legitimate outcomes.

Limit: These are context-specific author/project accounts, not randomised comparisons. Benchmark decomposition metrics are not operating outcomes.

Sources: ESA-S036, ESA-S037, ESA-S038, ESA-S039, ESA-S060, ESA-S063. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/15`.

### ESA-G17

**Architectural debt, incremental migration and continuous evaluation** — 1992 debt account; 2014 architecture-debt field study; 2019 guidance; 2024 revised migration essay.

Expedient choices and changing conditions can accumulate costs that a one-off design description conceals. Distinguish debt consequences, migrate through viable intermediate states and monitor selected architecture-related conditions.

Contribution: Architecture evaluation and revision can be continuous, and simplification can be an improvement.

Limit: Debt metaphors are not monetary measurements; fitness functions are selected proxies; migration adds coexistence costs.

Sources: ESA-S049, ESA-S050, ESA-S040, ESA-S043, ESA-S044. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/16`.

### ESA-G18

**Architecture for learned components** — 2015 engineering account and current continuation.

Data, feedback and undeclared consumers cross ordinary software boundaries. Treat data/configuration/feedback obligations as architectural dependencies and evaluate system behaviour, not code alone.

Contribution: A documented domain translation of dependency, interface and debt reasoning.

Limit: The 2015 account is practitioner evidence, not a controlled architecture intervention; generated structural proposals do not validate learned-system behaviour.

Sources: ESA-S056, ESA-S062. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/17`.

### ESA-G19

**Serverless and provider-managed architectural boundaries** — 2019 Berkeley report; 2024 energy-control study.

Invocation-managed platforms shift state, timing, resource and administrative assumptions. Design around provider execution boundaries and measure actual workload/cold-start/resource consequences.

Contribution: Serverless is an architectural translation with distinctive constraints, not an automatic simplification or sustainability guarantee.

Limit: The 2019 service descriptions are historical; no current price, quota or provider feature is inferred.

Sources: ESA-S057, ESA-S061. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/18`.

### ESA-G20

**Sustainability as an architecture concern** — 2024 comparative experiment; 2025 rapid meta-review.

Energy and resource use can be obscured by architecture proxies or isolated efficiency claims. Declare measurement boundary and workload, then consider energy with latency, reliability and other constraints.

Contribution: Architecture can contribute to sustainability while requiring broader lifecycle/context evidence.

Limit: Measured package/DRAM energy is not whole-lifecycle carbon; a rapid review and focus group do not prove universal prescriptions.

Sources: ESA-S061, ESA-S058. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/19`.

### ESA-G21

**LLM architectural assistance, adjacent automation comparisons and autonomy claims** — 2026 work inspected through2026-11-10; exact editions and access ceilings in the source/access records.

Generating alternatives and evaluating architectural material is costly; tools promise automation. Compare generated analyses/proposals with independent criteria and real-system evidence where available.

Contribution: Current work provides evidence about assistance and structural proposal tasks.

Limit: Small educational cases, one-run configurations and benchmark metrics do not establish dependable open-ended architecting.

Sources: ESA-S059, ESA-S062, ESA-S063, ESA-S067. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/20`.

### ESA-G22

**Driver-based recursive design and interleaved design/evaluation** — ADD v2.0 2006; general model 2007, online 2006.

A list of desirable qualities does not choose responsibilities, interfaces or useful decompositions. Choose drivers, select design concepts, decompose, allocate responsibilities and evaluate; iterate or recurse where uncertainty requires it.

Contribution: Makes architectural synthesis operative while allowing analysis, synthesis and evaluation to interleave.

Limit: Method-author synthesis and guidance are not independent comparative proof; recursion can become premature design expansion.

Sources: ESA-S024, ESA-S025, ESA-S016. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/21`.

### ESA-G23

**IEEE 1471 architecture-description recommendation** — 2000-10-09 publication.

Architecture descriptions need a common conceptual vocabulary and concern/viewpoint organisation. Standardise recommendations for describing software-intensive-system architecture.

Contribution: A description standard, not a quality certification.

Limit: Only official abstract and later history were inspected; no normative-clause claim is made.

Sources: ESA-S065, ESA-S013. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/22`.

### ESA-G24

**ISO/IEC/IEEE 42010 first edition** — 2011.

Continue harmonised architecture-description concepts. Specify an architecture-description framework rather than choosing a design for the user.

Contribution: An identifiable edition must be distinguished from its predecessors and successor.

Limit: Official abstract/metadata only; no clause-level comparison or compliance checklist is derived.

Sources: ESA-S012, ESA-S013. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/23`.

### ESA-G25

**ISO/IEC/IEEE 42010 second edition** — 2022.

Update the architecture-description conceptual framework. Relate entities, architecture, descriptions and stakeholder concerns within the standard’s stated scope.

Contribution: Maintains the distinction between architecture and architecture description.

Limit: Only official abstract and introductory preview were inspected; full normative clauses are an explicit access limit.

Sources: ESA-S011. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/24`.

### ESA-G26

**External function-behaviour-structure design model** — External work named in the inspected2007 generic-design paper.

Explain design as transformations among functions, behavior and structure. Hofmeister and colleagues explicitly adapt this outside model in their generic architectural design account.

Contribution: Documented external import prevents a purely internal or single-founder genealogy.

Limit: Original Gero source not independently read; this node records the receiving authors’ explicit attribution only, not a full study of that field.

Sources: ESA-S025. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/25`.

### ESA-G27

**External CSP process semantics** — Formalism explicitly used in the inspected September1998 Wright revision.

Specify interaction traces/failures and reason about composition. Wright represents ports, roles and glue using CSP-based semantics.

Contribution: A specific formal-language import supports a scoped connector theorem.

Limit: Original CSP publications are not independently read here; no whole formal-methods lineage is annexed.

Sources: ESA-S015. Selected pointer `/genealogy_nodes/26`.

## All historical relations

### ESA-GE01

`ESA-G01` → `ESA-G02` — `DOCUMENTED_INFLUENCE`.

Parnas1972 explicitly discusses Dijkstra’s hierarchy in Hierarchical Structure (p.1057, reference5) and distinguishes it from modular decomposition; Parnas1979 also discusses THE. This documents use/comparison of the hierarchy, not the exclusive origin of information hiding in THE.

Author assessment: Changed to narrowly documented influence. The exact Parnas1972 hierarchy discussion names Dijkstra while separating the mechanisms; this corrects the native absence-of-transmission assertion without making Dijkstra the exclusive founder.

Evidence boundary: Documentary influence is scoped to the explicitly discussed hierarchy; the different decomposition criterion and plural origins remain.

Sources: ESA-S005, ESA-S001, ESA-S006. Selected pointer `/genealogy_edges/0`.

### ESA-GE02

`ESA-G02` → `ESA-G12` — `ONLY_ANALOGOUS`.

Program-family and reusable-component strands have related commonality/responsibility concerns. This bundled-node comparison does not establish a shared historical ancestor: McIlroy’s1968 contribution precedes Parnas1972, and later collection descriptions do not document a single transmission chain. Parnas’s own family development remains within G02.

Author assessment: Changed to analogy for the bundled-node edge. Chronology and collection descriptions cannot prove shared ancestry across McIlroy, Parnas and later reuse; Parnas’s internal programme sequence remains separately documented.

Evidence boundary: The edge supports only the stated relationship; similarity does not prove exclusive ancestry.

Sources: ESA-S001, ESA-S006, ESA-S066. Selected pointer `/genealogy_edges/1`.

### ESA-GE03

`ESA-G03` → `ESA-G08` — `CONVERGENT_DEVELOPMENT`.

Rationalised design explanation and decision records address lost design knowledge through different forms; no exclusive direct derivation is claimed.

Author assessment: Retain convergence. Fresh Nygard text invokes Alexander by analogy and acknowledges Kruchten, but does not establish derivation from Parnas/Clements; shared rationale concerns are sufficient for comparison only.

Evidence boundary: The edge supports only the stated relationship; similarity does not prove exclusive ancestry.

Sources: ESA-S009, ESA-S027, ESA-S029. Selected pointer `/genealogy_edges/2`.

### ESA-GE04

`ESA-G04` → `ESA-G05` — `EXPLICIT_EXTENSION`.

Kruchten explicitly relates the 4+1 account to the elements/form/rationale architecture account and supplies multiple views.

Author assessment: Retain explicit extension. Kruchten directly uses Perry/Wolf’s formulation in introducing multiple views; his tailoring also defeats mandatory all-view appropriation.

Evidence boundary: The edge supports only the stated relationship; similarity does not prove exclusive ancestry.

Sources: ESA-S002, ESA-S003. Selected pointer `/genealogy_edges/3`.

Evidence boundary: The edge supports only the stated relationship; similarity does not prove exclusive ancestry.

## All inherited criticisms, independently reassessed

### ESA-CR001

**Objection:** “Architecture is important decisions” can become circular and give architects unlimited jurisdiction.

Source evidence: Foundational definitions differ; Fowler presents contextual definitions and competing architect roles, while Spolsky criticises detached generalisation.

Surviving response: Use consequence-bound significance and a named consumer rather than rank or diagram altitude.

Author assessment: The circularity objection defeats a universal jurisdiction claim, not every broad architecture definition. A decision-specific consequence is the surviving discriminator; no universal significance threshold is invented.

Residual: No universal threshold separates architecture from detailed design.

Affected claims: ESA-001, ESA-004, ESA-006, ESA-056. Sources: ESA-S002, ESA-S010, ESA-S042. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/0`.

### ESA-CR002

**Objection:** A component boundary or matching signature does not expose its control, state or environmental assumptions.

Source evidence: Parnas identifies a flaw in his illustrative boundary; the Aesop experience reports integration failures from architectural assumptions.

Surviving response: Combine information hiding with explicit semantic assumptions and appropriate dependency kinds.

Author assessment: The mismatch mechanism survives fresh passage inspection. Information hiding remains useful, but an interface must expose assumptions that consumers actually need; exposing every internal choice would defeat the original benefit.

Residual: Which assumptions to expose economically depends on credible changes and failures.

Affected claims: ESA-007, ESA-008, ESA-009, ESA-010, ESA-011, ESA-025. Sources: ESA-S001, ESA-S026. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/1`.

### ESA-CR003

**Objection:** More independently deployed components can increase coordination without yielding useful independence.

Source evidence: Istio’s 1.5 control-plane consolidation and Shopify’s modular-monolith account are positive alternatives; practitioner survey reports data and testing burdens.

Surviving response: Select or consolidate boundaries by actual scale, release, trust and change needs.

Author assessment: Fresh Istio and Fowler passages retain both alternatives and counterarguments. Reported consolidation reasons are meaningful evidence, while observed benefits remain contextual rather than randomized estimates.

Residual: Company accounts are selected, self-reported and not controlled economic comparisons.

Affected claims: ESA-012, ESA-049, ESA-050, ESA-051, ESA-056. Sources: ESA-S037, ESA-S038, ESA-S039, ESA-S060. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/2`.

### ESA-CR004

**Objection:** A well-run workshop can select the wrong population of scenarios or omit costly concerns.

Source evidence: QAW prioritisation is time-bounded; the risk-theme study found many omissions; controlled method comparison has narrow tasks.

Surviving response: Challenge scenario selection, preserve dissent and separate review outputs from operating evidence.

Author assessment: ATAM reports and the omission-theme corpus support actively seeking missing concerns. Their selected scenarios cannot establish open-world coverage; a missed high-consequence class is the relevant counter-test.

Residual: Reliable low-cost discovery of rare or unknown scenarios remains difficult.

Affected claims: ESA-002, ESA-014, ESA-015, ESA-028, ESA-031. Sources: ESA-S017, ESA-S020, ESA-S021, ESA-S023. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/3`.

### ESA-CR005

**Objection:** Quality objectives interact and apparent improvements can hide costs outside the chosen metric.

Source evidence: Tactic/utility models have assumptions; EcoFaaS improves selected energy/tail responses while increasing mean latency versus one baseline.

Surviving response: Declare hard constraints, measurement boundaries and accepted sacrifices.

Author assessment: Fresh EcoFaaS evidence includes the adverse average-latency comparison. A local Pareto improvement can still occur; rejection concerns independent maximization and concealed boundaries, not improvement itself.

Residual: Complete externality accounting and stable stakeholder utility are not generally available.

Affected claims: ESA-016, ESA-017, ESA-018, ESA-019, ESA-055. Sources: ESA-S016, ESA-S022, ESA-S061. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/4`.

### ESA-CR006

**Objection:** A complete-looking architecture description can be redundant, stale or consistently false.

Source evidence: Kruchten explicitly permits omission of irrelevant views; standards concern descriptions; reflexion requires mappings.

Surviving response: Choose concern-specific views and verify decision-relevant correspondence.

Author assessment: Kruchten’s own tailoring and ISO’s abstract scope support the critique. The remedy is useful, current correspondence, not mandatory extra diagrams or a claim that all descriptions are useless.

Residual: The cheapest sufficient representation and update mechanism vary by consumer and change rate.

Affected claims: ESA-020, ESA-021, ESA-023, ESA-037, ESA-059. Sources: ESA-S003, ESA-S011, ESA-S045. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/5`.

### ESA-CR007

**Objection:** Port compatibility alone does not preserve the deadlock property people may infer from it.

Source evidence: Wright’s author revision provides a non-conservative-glue counterexample and a theorem with additional conditions.

Surviving response: Require the theorem’s conservatism/deadlock-freedom conditions and separate realisation correspondence.

Author assessment: The formal response is decisive inside Wright’s semantics: keep all theorem premises. Outside the model, additional correspondence evidence is needed, not merely another proof-tool run.

Residual: Obtaining an adequate real-system abstraction can dominate the cost of proof.

Affected claims: ESA-022, ESA-068. Sources: ESA-S015. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/6`.

### ESA-CR008

**Objection:** Method structure, adoption and risk discovery do not independently establish predictive or economic superiority.

Source evidence: The internal replication uses 16 practitioner-students and finds no efficiency/ease advantage; ATAM risk reports are not downstream outcome trials.

Surviving response: Use a review proportional to the decision and test the consequences it predicts.

Author assessment: Native S023 details remain inherited; the fresh ATAM/CBAM passages independently establish limited method aims and assumptions. No lack of detected advantage is upgraded to proof of no benefit.

Residual: Independent longitudinal studies with credible counterfactuals are limited in the inspected evidence.

Affected claims: ESA-026, ESA-027, ESA-028, ESA-029, ESA-030. Sources: ESA-S021, ESA-S022, ESA-S023. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/7`.

### ESA-CR009

**Objection:** Decision records may be unread, stale or retroactive justifications.

Source evidence: Rationale survey respondents report barriers; rational reconstruction differs from actual history.

Surviving response: Capture only decision-relevant context, mark status/reopening conditions and distinguish reconstructed explanation.

Author assessment: Fresh Parnas/Clements and Nygard support useful rationale and status discipline. Reconstructed explanation must remain accurate; maintaining an exhaustive personal discovery diary is not the required alternative.

Residual: The total cost/benefit of documentation across turnover and distributed teams is not causally estimated here.

Affected claims: ESA-032, ESA-033, ESA-037, ESA-064. Sources: ESA-S009, ESA-S027, ESA-S029. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/8`.

### ESA-CR010

**Objection:** A deterministic Conway slogan confuses communication with hierarchy and association with cause.

Source evidence: Single-firm congruence and matched-product mirroring studies have selected populations, proxies and confounding.

Surviving response: Investigate the actual dependency and coordination mechanism before changing teams or architecture.

Author assessment: Conway’s homomorphism is not one-to-one determinism. Fresh Cataldo limitations support causal restraint while preserving the positive observed association and the practical coordination question.

Residual: Which coordination intervention causes benefit in a new setting requires new evidence.

Affected claims: ESA-004, ESA-005, ESA-034, ESA-035, ESA-036. Sources: ESA-S030, ESA-S031, ESA-S032. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/9`.

### ESA-CR011

**Objection:** Recovered structure and deviation counts do not establish harmful erosion or historical intent.

Source evidence: Reflexion is mapping-relative; review labels capture discussions; smell refactoring has heterogeneous effects.

Surviving response: Validate relations, connect deviations to current concerns and adjudicate baseline versus implementation.

Author assessment: Fresh smell and classifier studies contain positive bounded results. Those gains coexist with proxy, selection and attribution limits; the proper response is focused validation, not wholesale rejection of detection.

Residual: Ground-truth erosion and operational detector calibration remain unsettled.

Affected claims: ESA-024, ESA-038, ESA-039, ESA-040, ESA-041, ESA-042. Sources: ESA-S045, ESA-S046, ESA-S047, ESA-S048. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/10`.

### ESA-CR012

**Objection:** “Debt” can become a moral category for disliked design, with fictitious interest and compelled repayment.

Source evidence: The original metaphor and qualitative architecture-debt studies do not supply universal monetary rates.

Surviving response: Identify a compromise, specific causal burden, time horizon and feasible remedy/no-remedy alternative.

Author assessment: A debt claim must identify a causal future burden and feasible remedy. The financial metaphor neither makes all ugliness debt nor supplies numerical interest; qualitative evidence can still support prioritization.

Residual: Future interest and remediation cost are often uncertain or confounded with ordinary growth.

Affected claims: ESA-043, ESA-044, ESA-049. Sources: ESA-S049, ESA-S050, ESA-S056. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/11`.

### ESA-CR013

**Objection:** A self-adaptive system can silently lose a trustworthy model and still appear to be controlling correctly.

Source evidence: Rainbow fault-injection tests reveal silent invalid/stale state; industrial survey reports assurance and trust concerns.

Surviving response: Validate observation and action translation, constrain changes and evaluate actual post-change state; assess controller revisions separately.

Author assessment: Rainbow supplies silent-observation failures and positive cross-customization similarity. Industrial survey evidence broadens reported use but shares early respondents and cannot establish prevalence or unrestricted control reliability.

Residual: The inspected evidence does not justify unrestricted changing objectives or recursive self-authorisation.

Affected claims: ESA-045, ESA-046, ESA-047, ESA-048, ESA-063. Sources: ESA-S053, ESA-S054, ESA-S055. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/12`.

### ESA-CR014

**Objection:** Reusable families and semantic contexts can introduce incompatible variation and expensive translation.

Source evidence: Family-design mechanisms rely on useful subsets; mismatch exposes hidden assumptions; bounded contexts explicitly have relationships.

Surviving response: Limit family scope, test combinations and select shared meaning versus translation deliberately.

Author assessment: Family variability and semantic contexts address different objects. Explicit translation can solve a real problem while adding cost; neither conceptual analogy nor the failed fresh DDD fetch establishes historical descent.

Residual: Economics of family selection belong to later product-line inquiry, not a conclusion supplied by this lane.

Affected claims: ESA-013, ESA-049, ESA-061. Sources: ESA-S006, ESA-S026, ESA-S033. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/13`.

### ESA-CR015

**Objection:** Serverless and “green” labels can move rather than remove cost and resource consumption.

Source evidence: 2019 serverless analysis identifies storage/communication issues; EcoFaaS uses a narrow energy boundary and chosen SLO.

Surviving response: Use actual provider contracts and workload/lifecycle boundaries; compare feasible deployment alternatives.

Author assessment: Historical serverless analysis and present hardware experiments must keep separate temporal and measurement boundaries. No current vendor behavior, lifecycle carbon or social outcome follows automatically.

Residual: Lifecycle carbon, rebound and provider-hidden effects require additional target evidence.

Affected claims: ESA-052, ESA-055. Sources: ESA-S057, ESA-S058, ESA-S061. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/14`.

### ESA-CR016

**Objection:** Learned components can change behaviour through data and feedback without any source-level interface change.

Source evidence: ML engineering experience identifies entanglement, undeclared consumers and feedback loops.

Surviving response: Expand architectural dependencies and evaluation to data/model/population context.

Author assessment: The data/feedback mechanism is a substantive extension of code-only reasoning. It does not imply every model interaction is harmful; test the consequential dependency and its changing environment.

Residual: Unknown consumers and feedback may be difficult to detect; deployment benefit is not quantified here.

Affected claims: ESA-011, ESA-043, ESA-053. Sources: ESA-S056. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/15`.

### ESA-CR017

**Objection:** Automated text, diagram and clustering success can be mistaken for validated architecture or trustworthy metrics.

Source evidence: S047 studies labeled review-comment classification; S059/S062 study LLM evaluation or generated diagrams; S063 compares non-LLM decomposition with structural proxies; S067 supplies benchmark infrastructure. Their positive task results and limits differ, and none is representative end-to-end autonomy evidence.

Surviving response: Independently check generated semantics and evidence; use AI to propose rather than self-certify.

Author assessment: Separate LLM artifact tasks, classifiers, non-LLM decomposition and benchmark infrastructure. Positive bounded capabilities survive; generated confidence, structural scores and task aggregation do not settle autonomous operational reliability.

Residual: Representative longitudinal autonomy, total verification cost and changing requirements remain open.

Affected claims: ESA-024, ESA-040, ESA-054, ESA-060. Sources: ESA-S047, ESA-S059, ESA-S062, ESA-S063, ESA-S067. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/16`.

### ESA-CR018

**Objection:** Elaborate architectural process may burden a stable small system more than it protects it.

Source evidence: Original methods include tailoring; practitioner critiques document the forces favouring expediency and simpler forms.

Surviving response: Use the smallest coherent concern–decision–realisation–feedback arrangement, adding technique only for a demonstrated need.

Author assessment: Tailoring is supported by primary method discussions. A small system with a high-consequence external invariant may still need strong focused evidence, so headcount alone is not a nontrigger.

Residual: Evidence does not provide a universal small-team threshold or control-retirement formula.

Affected claims: ESA-056, ESA-058, ESA-067. Sources: ESA-S003, ESA-S008, ESA-S010, ESA-S017, ESA-S041, ESA-S042. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/17`.

### ESA-CR019

**Objection:** Usability cannot always be repaired in the presentation layer, but not every usability scenario has architectural consequences.

Source evidence: The usability/architecture scenario construction explicitly removed scenarios without substantive architectural implications.

Surviving response: Trace a user task to the state/control mechanism before moving responsibility across layers.

Author assessment: Cancellation demonstrates why a UI-only answer can fail. The source selected scenarios for architectural relevance; this does not license moving every usability concern into system architecture.

Residual: Architectural support is necessary in some cases but does not itself demonstrate usability for a population.

Affected claims: ESA-062, ESA-014, ESA-016. Sources: ESA-S018. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/18`.

### ESA-CR020

**Objection:** Weighted optimisation can conceal external obligations and stakeholder inequality.

Source evidence: CBAM’s additive utility assumptions and EcoFaaS’s chosen latency/energy boundary delimit their valid interpretations.

Surviving response: Treat hard constraints separately and disclose whose cost/benefit is measured; analytical extension, not a theorem supplied by either method.

Author assessment: A scalar objective can rank feasible choices only under justified aggregation. Hard constraints and whose cost is counted must remain explicit; technical analysis cannot decide every legitimate stakeholder conflict.

Residual: Resolving conflicting legitimate stakeholder constraints may require decisions outside software architecture.

Affected claims: ESA-029, ESA-047, ESA-055. Sources: ESA-S022, ESA-S061. Selected pointer `/criticism_ledger/19`.

## Historical causality and present synthesis

A criticism followed by an analytically useful response is not necessarily a documented historical cause of that response. The native evolution ledger preserves that distinction; current author reassessments accompany all20 entries. The same caution governs convergence, analogy and domain-translation edges. Specific documentary imports are confined to what the inspected receiving text actually names.

      "FORM": "Direct conversation, simple local design, one relevant alternative/quality check, and a short rationale only for a non-obvious durable choice.",
      "OMISSIONS": "No automatic ATAM event, ADL, multi-view package, service split, debt programme or adaptation controller. Ordinary engineering can supply the functions.",
      "ESCALATION_TRIGGER": "Growing consequence reach, turnover, unobserved risk or recurring cross-boundary change.",

      "AUTHOR_REQUALIFICATION": {

        "dependency_order": "Concerns and direct observations inform a local design choice; discussion, implementation and checking may iterate in any useful order.",
        "observation_need": "One meaningful interface/change/quality question and a real cheap alternative, including the current design.",
        "omission_test": "Removing ATAM, ADLs, view packages and a separate office leaves no unaddressed concern under the guard; removing the relevant contract check would lose protection.",
        "joint_cost": "Conversation, a small check and occasional rationale consume developer attention; even these are omitted when they lack a consumer. They share the same scarce developer time, so no independent net-benefit additions are assumed.",
        "failure_or_reversal": "A newly consequential external contract or repeated misunderstood changes defeats the small-scope assumption and warrants targeted additional evidence.",
        "cheaper_path": "Ordinary code review and direct knowledge may already realize the selected functions.",
